    56	/* Configuration ************************************************************/
    57	
    58	/* This driver does not support the SPI exchange method. */
    59	
    60	#ifdef CONFIG_SPI_EXCHANGE
    61	#  error "CONFIG_SPI_EXCHANGE must not be defined in the configuration"
    62	#endif
    63	
    64	/* SSP Clocking *************************************************************/
    65	
    66	#if defined(LPC176x)
    67	/* The CPU clock by 1, 2, 4, or 8 to get the SSP peripheral clock
    68	 * (SSP_CLOCK).  SSP_CLOCK may be further divided by 2-254 to get the SSP
    69	 * clock.  If we want a usable range of 4KHz to 25MHz for the SSP, then:
    70	 *
    71	 * 1. SSPCLK must be greater than (2*25MHz) = 50MHz, and
    72	 * 2. SSPCLK must be less than (254*40Khz) = 101.6MHz.
    73	 *
    74	 * If we assume that CCLK less than or equal to 100MHz, we can just
    75	 * use the CCLK undivided to get the SSP_CLOCK.
    76	 */
    77	
    78	#  if LPC17_40_CCLK > 100000000
    79	#    error "CCLK <= 100,000,000 assumed"
    80	#  endif
    81	
    82	#  define SSP_PCLKSET_DIV    SYSCON_PCLKSEL_CCLK
    83	#  define SSP_CLOCK          LPC17_40_CCLK
    84	
    85	#elif defined(LPC178x_40xx)
    86	/* All peripherals are clocked by the same peripheral clock in the
    87	 * LPC178x/40xx family.
    88	 */
    89	
    90	#  define SSP_CLOCK          BOARD_PCLK_FREQUENCY
    91	
    92	#endif
    93	
    94	/****************************************************************************
    95	 * Private Types
    96	 ****************************************************************************/
    97	
    98	/* This structure describes the state of the SSP driver */
    99	
   100	struct lpc17_40_sspdev_s
   101	{
   102	  struct spi_dev_s spidev;     /* Externally visible part of the SPI interface */
   103	  uint32_t         sspbase;    /* SPIn base address */
   104	#ifdef CONFIG_LPC17_40_SSP_INTERRUPTS
   105	  uint8_t          sspirq;     /* SPI IRQ number */
   106	#endif
   107	  mutex_t          lock;       /* Held while chip is selected for mutual exclusion */
   108	  uint32_t         frequency;  /* Requested clock frequency */
   109	  uint32_t         actual;     /* Actual clock frequency */
   110	  uint8_t          nbits;      /* Width of word in bits (4 to 16) */
   111	  uint8_t          mode;       /* Mode 0,1,2,3 */
   112	};
   113

   265	/****************************************************************************
   266	 * Public Data
   267	 ****************************************************************************/
   268	
   269	/****************************************************************************
   270	 * Private Functions
   271	 ****************************************************************************/
   272	
   273	/****************************************************************************
   274	 * Name: ssp_getreg
   275	 *
   276	 * Description:
   277	 *   Get the contents of the SPI register at offset
   278	 *
   279	 * Input Parameters:
   280	 *   priv   - private SPI device structure
   281	 *   offset - offset to the register of interest
   282	 *
   283	 * Returned Value:
   284	 *   The contents of the 32-bit register
   285	 *
   286	 ****************************************************************************/
   287	
   288	static inline uint32_t ssp_getreg(struct lpc17_40_sspdev_s *priv,
   289	                                  uint8_t offset)
   290	{
   291	  return getreg32(priv->sspbase + (uint32_t)offset);
   292	}
   293	
   294	/****************************************************************************
   295	 * Name: ssp_putreg
   296	 *
   297	 * Description:
   298	 *   Write a 32-bit value to the SPI register at offset
   299	 *
   300	 * Input Parameters:
   301	 *   priv   - private SPI device structure
   302	 *   offset - offset to the register of interest
   303	 *   value  - the 16-bit value to be written
   304	 *
   305	 * Returned Value:
   306	 *   None
   307	 *
   308	 ****************************************************************************/
   309	
   310	static inline void ssp_putreg(struct lpc17_40_sspdev_s *priv,
   311	                              uint8_t offset, uint32_t value)
   312	{
   313	  putreg32(value, priv->sspbase + (uint32_t)offset);
   314	}
   315	
   316	/****************************************************************************
   317	 * Name: ssp_lock
   318	 *
   319	 * Description:
   320	 *   On SPI buses where there are multiple devices, it will be necessary to
   321	 *   lock SPI to have exclusive access to the buses for a sequence of
   322	 *   transfers.  The bus should be locked before the chip is selected. After
   323	 *   locking the SPI bus, the caller should then also call the setfrequency,
   324	 *   setbits, and setmode methods to make sure that the SPI is properly
   325	 *   configured for the device.  If the SPI bus is being shared, then it
   326	 *   may have been left in an incompatible state.
   327	 *
   328	 * Input Parameters:
   329	 *   dev  - Device-specific state data
   330	 *   lock - true: Lock spi bus, false: unlock SPI bus
   331	 *
   332	 * Returned Value:
   333	 *   None
   334	 *
   335	 ****************************************************************************/
   336	
   337	static int ssp_lock(struct spi_dev_s *dev, bool lock)
   338	{
   339	  struct lpc17_40_sspdev_s *priv = (struct lpc17_40_sspdev_s *)dev;
   340	  int ret;
   341	
   342	  if (lock)
   343	    {
   344	      ret = nxmutex_lock(&priv->lock);
   345	    }
   346	  else
   347	    {
   348	      ret = nxmutex_unlock(&priv->lock);
   349	    }
   350	
   351	  return ret;
   352	}
   353	
   354	/****************************************************************************
   355	 * Name: ssp_setfrequency
   356	 *
   357	 * Description:
   358	 *   Set the SPI frequency.
   359	 *
   360	 * Input Parameters:
   361	 *   dev -       Device-specific state data
   362	 *   frequency - The SPI frequency requested
   363	 *
   364	 * Returned Value:
   365	 *   Returns the actual frequency selected
   366	 *
   367	 ****************************************************************************/
   368	
   369	static uint32_t ssp_setfrequency(struct spi_dev_s *dev,
   370	                                 uint32_t frequency)
   371	{
   372	  struct lpc17_40_sspdev_s *priv = (struct lpc17_40_sspdev_s *)dev;
   373	  uint32_t cpsdvsr;
   374	  uint32_t scr;
   375	  uint32_t regval;
   376	  uint32_t actual;
   377	
   378	  DEBUGASSERT(priv && frequency <= SSP_CLOCK / 2);
   379	
   380	  /* Check if the requested frequency is the same as the frequency
   381	   * selection.
   382	   */
   383	
   384	  if (priv->frequency == frequency)
   385	    {
   386	      /* We are already at this frequency.  Return the actual. */
   387	
   388	      return priv->actual;
   389	    }
   390	
   391	  /* The SSP bit frequency is given by:
   392	   *
   393	   *   frequency = SSP_CLOCK / (CPSDVSR * (SCR+1)).
   394	   *
   395	   * Let's try for a solution with the smallest value of SCR.  NOTES:
   396	   * (1) In the calculations below, the value of the variable 'scr' is
   397	   * (SCR+1) in the above equation. (2) On slower LPC17xx/LPC40xx parts, SCR
   398	   * will probably always be zero.
   399	   */
   400	
   401	  for (scr = 1; scr <= 256; scr++)
   402	    {
   403	      /* CPSDVSR = SSP_CLOCK / (SCR + 1) / frequency */
   404	
   405	      cpsdvsr = SSP_CLOCK / (scr * frequency);
   406	
   407	      /* Break out on the first solution we find with the smallest value
   408	       * of SCR and with CPSDVSR within the maximum range or 254.
   409	       */
   410	
   411	      if (cpsdvsr < 255)
   412	        {
   413	          break;
   414	        }
   415	    }
   416	
   417	  DEBUGASSERT(scr <= 256 && cpsdvsr <= 255);
   418	
   419	  /* "In master mode, CPSDVSRmin = 2 or larger (even numbers only)" */
   420	
   421	  if (cpsdvsr < 2)
   422	    {
   423	      /* Clip to the minimum value. */
   424	
   425	      cpsdvsr = 2;
   426	    }
   427	  else if (cpsdvsr > 254)
   428	    {
   429	      /* This should never happen */
   430	
   431	      cpsdvsr = 254;
   432	    }
   433	
   434	  /* Force even */
   435	
   436	  cpsdvsr = (cpsdvsr + 1) & ~1;
   437	
   438	  /* Save the new CPSDVSR and SCR values */
   439	
   440	  ssp_putreg(priv, LPC17_40_SSP_CPSR_OFFSET, cpsdvsr);
   441	
   442	  regval  = ssp_getreg(priv, LPC17_40_SSP_CR0_OFFSET);
   443	  regval &= ~SSP_CR0_SCR_MASK;
   444	  regval |= ((scr - 1) << SSP_CR0_SCR_SHIFT);
   445	  ssp_putreg(priv, LPC17_40_SSP_CR0_OFFSET, regval);
   446	
   447	  /* Calculate the new actual */
   448	
   449	  actual = SSP_CLOCK / (cpsdvsr * scr);
   450	
   451	  /* Save the frequency setting */
   452	
   453	  priv->frequency = frequency;
   454	  priv->actual    = actual;
   455	
   456	  spiinfo("Frequency %" PRId32 "->%" PRId32 "\n", frequency, actual);
   457	  return actual;
   458	}
   459	
   460	/****************************************************************************
   461	 * Name: ssp_setmode
   462	 *
   463	 * Description:
   464	 *   Set the SPI mode. Optional.  See enum spi_mode_e for mode definitions
   465	 *
   466	 * Input Parameters:
   467	 *   dev -  Device-specific state data
   468	 *   mode - The SPI mode requested
   469	 *
   470	 * Returned Value:
   471	 *   none
   472	 *
   473	 ****************************************************************************/
   474	
   475	static void ssp_setmode(struct spi_dev_s *dev, enum spi_mode_e mode)
   476	{
   477	  struct lpc17_40_sspdev_s *priv = (struct lpc17_40_sspdev_s *)dev;
   478	  uint32_t regval;
   479	
   480	  /* Has the mode changed? */
   481	
   482	  if (mode != priv->mode)
   483	    {
   484	      /* Yes... Set CR0 appropriately */
   485	
   486	      regval = ssp_getreg(priv, LPC17_40_SSP_CR0_OFFSET);
   487	      regval &= ~(SSP_CR0_CPOL | SSP_CR0_CPHA);
   488	
   489	      switch (mode)
   490	        {
   491	        case SPIDEV_MODE0: /* CPOL=0; CPHA=0 */
   492	          break;
   493	
   494	        case SPIDEV_MODE1: /* CPOL=0; CPHA=1 */
   495	          regval |= SSP_CR0_CPHA;
   496	          break;
   497	
   498	        case SPIDEV_MODE2: /* CPOL=1; CPHA=0 */
   499	          regval |= SSP_CR0_CPOL;
   500	          break;
   501	
   502	        case SPIDEV_MODE3: /* CPOL=1; CPHA=1 */
   503	          regval |= (SSP_CR0_CPOL | SSP_CR0_CPHA);
   504	          break;
   505	
   506	        default:
   507	          spierr("ERROR: Bad mode: %d\n", mode);
   508	          DEBUGASSERT(FALSE);
   509	          return;
   510	        }
   511	
   512	      ssp_putreg(priv, LPC17_40_SSP_CR0_OFFSET, regval);
   513	
   514	      /* Save the mode so that subsequent re-configurations will be faster */
   515	
   516	      priv->mode = mode;
   517	    }
   518	}
   519	
   520	/****************************************************************************
   521	 * Name: ssp_setbits
   522	 *
   523	 * Description:
   524	 *   Set the number if bits per word.
   525	 *
   526	 * Input Parameters:
   527	 *   dev -  Device-specific state data
   528	 *   nbits - The number of bits requested
   529	 *
   530	 * Returned Value:
   531	 *   none
   532	 *
   533	 ****************************************************************************/
   534	
   535	static void ssp_setbits(struct spi_dev_s *dev, int nbits)
   536	{
   537	  struct lpc17_40_sspdev_s *priv = (struct lpc17_40_sspdev_s *)dev;
   538	  uint32_t regval;
   539	
   540	  /* Has the number of bits changed? */
   541	
   542	  DEBUGASSERT(priv && nbits > 3 && nbits < 17);
   543	
   544	  if (nbits != priv->nbits)
   545	    {
   546	      /* Yes... Set CR1 appropriately */
   547	
   548	      regval = ssp_getreg(priv, LPC17_40_SSP_CR0_OFFSET);
   549	      regval &= ~SSP_CR0_DSS_MASK;
   550	      regval |= ((nbits - 1) << SSP_CR0_DSS_SHIFT);
   551	      ssp_putreg(priv, LPC17_40_SSP_CR0_OFFSET, regval);
   552	
   553	      /* Save the selection so that subsequent re-configurations will be
   554	       * faster.
   555	       */
   556	
   557	      priv->nbits = nbits;
   558	    }
   559	}
   560	
   561	/****************************************************************************
   562	 * Name: ssp_send
   563	 *
   564	 * Description:
   565	 *   Exchange one word on SPI
   566	 *
   567	 * Input Parameters:
   568	 *   dev - Device-specific state data
   569	 *   wd  - The word to send.  the size of the data is determined by the
   570	 *         number of bits selected for the SPI interface.
   571	 *
   572	 * Returned Value:
   573	 *   response
   574	 *
   575	 ****************************************************************************/
   576	
   577	static uint32_t ssp_send(struct spi_dev_s *dev, uint32_t wd)
   578	{
   579	  struct lpc17_40_sspdev_s *priv = (struct lpc17_40_sspdev_s *)dev;
   580	  register uint32_t regval;
   581	
   582	  /* Wait while the TX FIFO is full */
   583	
   584	  while (!(ssp_getreg(priv, LPC17_40_SSP_SR_OFFSET) & SSP_SR_TNF));
   585	
   586	  /* Write the byte to the TX FIFO */
   587	
   588	  ssp_putreg(priv, LPC17_40_SSP_DR_OFFSET, wd);
   589	
   590	  /* Wait for the RX FIFO not empty */
   591	
   592	  while (!(ssp_getreg(priv, LPC17_40_SSP_SR_OFFSET) & SSP_SR_RNE));
   593	
   594	  /* Get the value from the RX FIFO and return it */
   595	
   596	  regval = ssp_getreg(priv, LPC17_40_SSP_DR_OFFSET);
   597	  spiinfo("%04" PRId32 "->%04" PRId32 "\n", wd, regval);
   598	  return regval;
   599	}
   600	
   601	/****************************************************************************
   602	 * Name: ssp_sndblock
   603	 *
   604	 * Description:
   605	 *   Send a block of data on SPI
   606	 *
   607	 * Input Parameters:
   608	 *   dev -    Device-specific state data
   609	 *   buffer - A pointer to the buffer of data to be sent
   610	 *   nwords - the length of data to send from the buffer in number of words.
   611	 *            The wordsize is determined by the number of bits-per-word
   612	 *            selected for the SPI interface.  If nbits <= 8, the data is
   613	 *            packed into uint8_t's; if nbits >8, the data is packed into
   614	 *            uint16_t's
   615	 *
   616	 * Returned Value:
   617	 *   None
   618	 *
   619	 ****************************************************************************/
   620	
   621	static void ssp_sndblock(struct spi_dev_s *dev, const void *buffer,
   622	                         size_t nwords)
   623	{
   624	  struct lpc17_40_sspdev_s *priv = (struct lpc17_40_sspdev_s *)dev;
   625	  union
   626	  {
   627	    const uint8_t  *p8;
   628	    const uint16_t *p16;
   629	    const void     *pv;
   630	  } u;
   631	
   632	  uint32_t data;
   633	  uint32_t sr;
   634	
   635	  /* Loop while there are bytes remaining to be sent */
   636	
   637	  spiinfo("nwords: %d\n", nwords);
   638	  u.pv = buffer;
   639	  while (nwords > 0)
   640	    {
   641	      /* While the TX FIFO is not full and there are bytes left to send */
   642	
   643	      while ((ssp_getreg(priv, LPC17_40_SSP_SR_OFFSET) & SSP_SR_TNF) &&
   644	             nwords)
   645	        {
   646	          /* Fetch the data to send */
   647	
   648	          if (priv->nbits > 8)
   649	            {
   650	              data = (uint32_t)*u.p16++;
   651	            }
   652	          else
   653	            {
   654	              data = (uint32_t)*u.p8++;
   655	            }
   656	
   657	          /* Send the data */
   658	
   659	          ssp_putreg(priv, LPC17_40_SSP_DR_OFFSET, data);
   660	          nwords--;
   661	        }
   662	    }
   663	
   664	  /* Then discard all card responses until the RX & TX FIFOs are emptied. */
   665	
   666	  spiinfo("discarding\n");
   667	  do
   668	    {
   669	      /* Is there anything in the RX fifo? */
   670	
   671	      sr = ssp_getreg(priv, LPC17_40_SSP_SR_OFFSET);
   672	      if ((sr & SSP_SR_RNE) != 0)
   673	        {
   674	          /* Yes.. Read and discard */
   675	
   676	          ssp_getreg(priv, LPC17_40_SSP_DR_OFFSET);
   677	        }
   678	
   679	      /* There is a race condition where TFE may go true just before
   680	       * RNE goes true and this loop terminates prematurely.  The nasty
   681	       * little delay in the following solves that (it could probably be
   682	       * tuned to improve performance).
   683	       */
   684	
   685	      else if ((sr & SSP_SR_TFE) != 0)
   686	        {
   687	          up_udelay(100);
   688	          sr = ssp_getreg(priv, LPC17_40_SSP_SR_OFFSET);
   689	        }
   690	    }
   691	  while ((sr & SSP_SR_RNE) != 0 || (sr & SSP_SR_TFE) == 0);
   692	}
   693	
   694	/****************************************************************************
   695	 * Name: ssp_recvblock
   696	 *
   697	 * Description:
   698	 *   Revice a block of data from SPI
   699	 *
   700	 * Input Parameters:
   701	 *   dev -    Device-specific state data
   702	 *   buffer - A pointer to the buffer in which to receive data
   703	 *   nwords - the length of data that can be received in the buffer in number
   704	 *            of words.  The wordsize is determined by the number of
   705	 *            bits-per-word selected for the SPI interface.  If nbits <= 8,
   706	 *            the data is packed into uint8_t's; if nbits >8, the data is
   707	 *            packed into uint16_t's
   708	 *
   709	 * Returned Value:
   710	 *   None
   711	 *
   712	 ****************************************************************************/
   713	
   714	static void ssp_recvblock(struct spi_dev_s *dev, void *buffer,
   715	                          size_t nwords)
   716	{
   717	  struct lpc17_40_sspdev_s *priv = (struct lpc17_40_sspdev_s *)dev;
   718	  union
   719	  {
   720	    uint8_t  *p8;
   721	    uint16_t *p16;
   722	    void     *pv;
   723	  } u;
   724	
   725	  uint32_t data;
   726	  uint32_t rxpending = 0;
   727	
   728	  /* While there is remaining to be sent (and no synchronization error
   729	   * has occurred)
   730	   */
   731	
   732	  spiinfo("nwords: %zd\n", nwords);
   733	  u.pv = buffer;
   734	  while (nwords || rxpending)
   735	    {
   736	      /* Fill the transmit FIFO with 0xffff...
   737	       * Write 0xff to the data register while (1) the TX FIFO is
   738	       * not full, (2) we have not exceeded the depth of the TX FIFO,
   739	       * and (3) there are more bytes to be sent.
   740	       */
   741	
   742	      spiinfo("TX: rxpending: %" PRId32 " nwords: %zd\n", rxpending, nwords);
   743	      while ((ssp_getreg(priv, LPC17_40_SSP_SR_OFFSET) & SSP_SR_TNF) &&
   744	             (rxpending < LPC17_40_SSP_FIFOSZ) && nwords)
   745	        {
   746	          ssp_putreg(priv, LPC17_40_SSP_DR_OFFSET, 0xffff);
   747	          nwords--;
   748	          rxpending++;
   749	        }
   750	
   751	      /* Now, read the RX data from the RX FIFO while the RX FIFO is
   752	       * not empty.
   753	       */
   754	
   755	      spiinfo("RX: rxpending: %" PRId32 "\n", rxpending);
   756	      while (ssp_getreg(priv, LPC17_40_SSP_SR_OFFSET) & SSP_SR_RNE)
   757	        {
   758	          data = (uint8_t)ssp_getreg(priv, LPC17_40_SSP_DR_OFFSET);
   759	          if (priv->nbits > 8)
   760	            {
   761	              *u.p16++ = (uint16_t)data;
   762	            }
   763	          else
   764	            {
   765	              *u.p8++  = (uint8_t)data;
   766	            }
   767	
   768	          rxpending--;
   769	        }
   770	    }
   771	}
   772	
   773	/****************************************************************************
   774	 * Name: lpc17_40_ssp0initialize
   775	 *
   776	 * Description:
   777	 *   Initialize the SSP0
   778	 *
   779	 * Input Parameters:
   780	 *   None
   781	 *
   782	 * Returned Value:
   783	 *   Valid SPI device structure reference on success; a NULL on failure
   784	 *
   785	 ****************************************************************************/
   786	
   787	#ifdef CONFIG_LPC17_40_SSP0
   788	static inline struct lpc17_40_sspdev_s *lpc17_40_ssp0initialize(void)
   789	{
   790	  irqstate_t flags;
   791	  uint32_t regval;
   792	
   793	  /* Configure multiplexed pins as connected on the board.  Chip select
   794	   * pins must be configured by board-specific logic.  All SSP0 pins and
   795	   * one SSP1 pin (SCK) have multiple, alternative pin selection.
   796	   * Definitions in the board.h file must be provided to resolve the
   797	   * board-specific pin configuration like:
   798	   *
   799	   * #define GPIO_SSP0_SCK GPIO_SSP0_SCK_1
   800	   */
   801	
   802	  flags = enter_critical_section();
   803	  lpc17_40_configgpio(GPIO_SSP0_SCK);
   804	  lpc17_40_configgpio(GPIO_SSP0_MISO);
   805	  lpc17_40_configgpio(GPIO_SSP0_MOSI);
   806	
   807	  /* Configure clocking */
   808	
   809	#ifdef LPC176x
   810	  regval  = getreg32(LPC17_40_SYSCON_PCLKSEL1);
   811	  regval &= ~SYSCON_PCLKSEL1_SSP0_MASK;
   812	  regval |= (SSP_PCLKSET_DIV << SYSCON_PCLKSEL1_SSP0_SHIFT);
   813	  putreg32(regval, LPC17_40_SYSCON_PCLKSEL1);
   814	#endif
   815	
   816	  /* Enable peripheral clocking to SSP0 */
   817	
   818	  regval  = getreg32(LPC17_40_SYSCON_PCONP);
   819	  regval |= SYSCON_PCONP_PCSSP0;
   820	  putreg32(regval, LPC17_40_SYSCON_PCONP);
   821	  leave_critical_section(flags);
   822	
   823	  return &g_ssp0dev;
   824	}
   825	#endif
   826	
   827	/****************************************************************************
   828	 * Name: lpc17_40_ssp1initialize
   829	 *
   830	 * Description:
   831	 *   Initialize the SSP1
   832	 *
   833	 * Input Parameters:
   834	 *   None
   835	 *
   836	 * Returned Value:
   837	 *   Valid SPI device structure reference on success; a NULL on failure
   838	 *
   839	 ****************************************************************************/
   840	
   841	#ifdef CONFIG_LPC17_40_SSP1
   842	static inline struct lpc17_40_sspdev_s *lpc17_40_ssp1initialize(void)
   843	{
   844	  irqstate_t flags;
   845	  uint32_t regval;
   846	
   847	  /* Configure multiplexed pins as connected on the board.  Chip select
   848	   * pins must be configured by board-specific logic.  All SSP0 pins and
   849	   * one SSP1 pin (SCK) have multiple, alternative pin selection.
   850	   * Definitions in the board.h file must be provided to resolve the
   851	   * board-specific pin configuration like:
   852	   *
   853	   * #define GPIO_SSP0_SCK GPIO_SSP0_SCK_1
   854	   */
   855	
   856	  flags = enter_critical_section();
   857	  lpc17_40_configgpio(GPIO_SSP1_SCK);
   858	  lpc17_40_configgpio(GPIO_SSP1_MISO);
   859	  lpc17_40_configgpio(GPIO_SSP1_MOSI);
   860	
   861	  /* Configure clocking */
   862	
   863	#ifdef LPC176x
   864	  regval  = getreg32(LPC17_40_SYSCON_PCLKSEL0);
   865	  regval &= ~SYSCON_PCLKSEL0_SSP1_MASK;
   866	  regval |= (SSP_PCLKSET_DIV << SYSCON_PCLKSEL0_SSP1_SHIFT);
   867	  putreg32(regval, LPC17_40_SYSCON_PCLKSEL0);
   868	#endif
   869	
   870	  /* Enable peripheral clocking to SSP0 and SSP1 */
   871	
   872	  regval  = getreg32(LPC17_40_SYSCON_PCONP);
   873	  regval |= SYSCON_PCONP_PCSSP1;
   874	  putreg32(regval, LPC17_40_SYSCON_PCONP);
   875	  leave_critical_section(flags);
   876	
   877	  return &g_ssp1dev;
   878	}
   879	#endif
   880	
   881	/****************************************************************************
   882	 * Name: lpc17_40_ssp2initialize
   883	 *
   884	 * Description:
   885	 *   Initialize the SSP2
   886	 *
   887	 * Input Parameters:
   888	 *   None
   889	 *
   890	 * Returned Value:
   891	 *   Valid SPI device structure reference on success; a NULL on failure
   892	 *
   893	 ****************************************************************************/
   894	
   895	#ifdef CONFIG_LPC17_40_SSP2
   896	static inline struct lpc17_40_sspdev_s *lpc17_40_ssp2initialize(void)
   897	{
   898	  irqstate_t flags;
   899	  uint32_t regval;
   900	
   901	  /* Configure multiplexed pins as connected on the board.  Chip select
   902	   * pins must be configured by board-specific logic.  All SSP2 pins have
   903	   * multiple, alternative pin selection. Definitions in the board.h file
   904	   * must be provided to resolve the board-specific pin configuration like:
   905	   *
   906	   * #define GPIO_SSP2_SCK GPIO_SSP2_SCK_1
   907	   */
   908	
   909	  flags = enter_critical_section();
   910	  lpc17_40_configgpio(GPIO_SSP2_SCK);
   911	  lpc17_40_configgpio(GPIO_SSP2_MISO);
   912	  lpc17_40_configgpio(GPIO_SSP2_MOSI);
   913	
   914	  /* Configure clocking */
   915	
   916	#ifdef LPC176x
   917	  regval  = getreg32(LPC17_40_SYSCON_PCLKSEL0);
   918	  regval &= ~SYSCON_PCLKSEL0_SSP2_MASK;
   919	  regval |= (SSP_PCLKSET_DIV << SYSCON_PCLKSEL0_SSP2_SHIFT);
   920	  putreg32(regval, LPC17_40_SYSCON_PCLKSEL0);
   921	#endif
   922	
   923	  /* Enable peripheral clocking to SSP0 and SSP1 */
   924	
   925	  regval  = getreg32(LPC17_40_SYSCON_PCONP);
   926	  regval |= SYSCON_PCONP_PCSSP2;
   927	  putreg32(regval, LPC17_40_SYSCON_PCONP);
   928	  leave_critical_section(flags);
   929	
   930	  return &g_ssp2dev;
   931	}
   932	#endif
   933	
   934	/****************************************************************************
   935	 * Public Functions
   936	 ****************************************************************************/
   937	
   938	/****************************************************************************
   939	 * Name: lpc17_40_sspbus_initialize
   940	 *
   941	 * Description:
   942	 *   Initialize the selected SSP port.
   943	 *
   944	 * Input Parameters:
   945	 *   Port number (for hardware that has multiple SPI interfaces)
   946	 *
   947	 * Returned Value:
   948	 *   Valid SPI device structure reference on success; a NULL on failure
   949	 *
   950	 ****************************************************************************/
   951	
   952	struct spi_dev_s *lpc17_40_sspbus_initialize(int port)
   953	{
   954	  struct lpc17_40_sspdev_s *priv;
   955	  uint32_t regval;
   956	  int i;
   957	
   958	  /* Only the SSP0 and SSP1 interfaces are supported */
   959	
   960	  switch (port)
   961	    {
   962	#ifdef CONFIG_LPC17_40_SSP0
   963	    case 0:
   964	      priv = lpc17_40_ssp0initialize();
   965	      break;
   966	#endif
   967	#ifdef CONFIG_LPC17_40_SSP1
   968	    case 1:
   969	      priv = lpc17_40_ssp1initialize();
   970	      break;
   971	#endif
   972	#ifdef CONFIG_LPC17_40_SSP2
   973	    case 2:
   974	      priv = lpc17_40_ssp2initialize();
   975	      break;
   976	#endif
   977	    default:
   978	      return NULL;
   979	    }
   980	
   981	  /* Configure 8-bit SPI mode */
   982	
   983	  ssp_putreg(priv, LPC17_40_SSP_CR0_OFFSET,
   984	             SSP_CR0_DSS_8BIT | SSP_CR0_FRF_SPI);
   985	
   986	  /* Disable the SSP and all interrupts (we'll poll for all data) */
   987	
   988	  ssp_putreg(priv, LPC17_40_SSP_CR1_OFFSET, 0);
   989	  ssp_putreg(priv, LPC17_40_SSP_IMSC_OFFSET, 0);
   990	
   991	  /* Set the initial SSP configuration */
   992	
   993	  priv->frequency = 0;
   994	  priv->nbits     = 8;
   995	  priv->mode      = SPIDEV_MODE0;
   996	
   997	  /* Select a default frequency of approx. 400KHz */
   998	
   999	  ssp_setfrequency((struct spi_dev_s *)priv, 400000);
  1000	
  1001	  /* Enable the SPI */
  1002	
  1003	  regval = ssp_getreg(priv, LPC17_40_SSP_CR1_OFFSET);
  1004	  ssp_putreg(priv, LPC17_40_SSP_CR1_OFFSET, regval | SSP_CR1_SSE);
  1005	  for (i = 0; i < LPC17_40_SSP_FIFOSZ; i++)
  1006	    {
  1007	      ssp_getreg(priv, LPC17_40_SSP_DR_OFFSET);
  1008	    }
  1009	
  1010	  return &priv->spidev;
  1011	}
  1012	
  1013	/****************************************************************************
  1014	 * Name: ssp_flush
  1015	 *
  1016	 * Description:
  1017	 *   Flush and discard any words left in the RX fifo.  This can be done
  1018	 *   after a device is deselected if you worry about such things.
  1019	 *
  1020	 * Input Parameters:
  1021	 *   dev - Device-specific state data
  1022	 *
  1023	 * Returned Value:
  1024	 *   None
  1025	 *
  1026	 ****************************************************************************/
  1027	
  1028	void ssp_flush(struct spi_dev_s *dev)
  1029	{
  1030	  struct lpc17_40_sspdev_s *priv = (struct lpc17_40_sspdev_s *)dev;
  1031	
  1032	  /* Wait for the TX FIFO not full indication */
  1033	
  1034	  while (!(ssp_getreg(priv, LPC17_40_SSP_SR_OFFSET) & SSP_SR_TNF));
  1035	  ssp_putreg(priv, LPC17_40_SSP_DR_OFFSET, 0xff);
  1036	
  1037	  /* Wait until TX FIFO and TX shift buffer are empty */
  1038	
  1039	  while (ssp_getreg(priv, LPC17_40_SSP_SR_OFFSET) & SSP_SR_BSY);
  1040	
  1041	  /* Wait until RX FIFO is not empty */
  1042	
  1043	  while (!(ssp_getreg(priv, LPC17_40_SSP_SR_OFFSET) & SSP_SR_RNE));
  1044	
  1045	  /* Then read and discard bytes until the RX FIFO is empty */
  1046	
  1047	  do
  1048	    {
  1049	      ssp_getreg(priv, LPC17_40_SSP_DR_OFFSET);
  1050	    }
  1051	  while (ssp_getreg(priv, LPC17_40_SSP_SR_OFFSET) & SSP_SR_RNE);
  1052	}
  1053	
  1054	#endif /* CONFIG_LPC17_40_SSP0/1 */

    47		depends on SCHED_INSTRUMENTATION_DUMP

    49			If this option is enabled, syslog will be redirected to sched_note,
    50			It uses the sched_note_printf macro to replace syslog
    51	
    52	config SYSLOG_NONE
    53		bool "Disable system logging"
    54		---help---
    55			System logging is disabled and all calls to syslog() are replaced with
    56			an empty function. This option can be useful for small systems when we don't
    57			have any logging support, so we can get rid of unused logic.
    58			This way the final image also won't contain the strings that are present
    59			in syslog().
    60	
    61	endchoice
    62	
    63	config SYSLOG_CHARDEV
    64		bool "SYSLOG character device"
    65		default n
    66		---help---
    67			Enables support for a simple character driver at /dev/log whose
    68			write() method will transfer data to the SYSLOG device.  This can be
    69			useful if, for example, you want to redirect the output of a program
    70			to the SYSLOG.
    71	
    72			NOTE that unlike other syslog output, this data is unformatted raw
    73			byte output with no time-stamping or any other SYSLOG features
    74			supported.
    75	
    76	config SYSLOG_DEFAULT_MASK
    77		hex "syslog mask default value"
    78		default 0xff
    79	
    80	config SYSLOG_OPENLOG
    81		bool "openlog() ident and options"
    82		default !DEFAULT_SMALL
    83		depends on SYSLOG
    84		---help---
    85			Keep the ident and the options that openlog() sets, and put
    86			"ident: " ("ident[pid]: " with LOG_PID) before the messages of the
    87			process, and copy them to stderr with LOG_PERROR.  If disabled,
    88			openlog() and closelog() still exist but do nothing, and the
    89			messages have no ident, as when a program never calls openlog().
    90	
    91	if SYSLOG
    92	comment "SYSLOG options"
    93	
    94	config SYSLOG_CRLF
    95		bool "Syslog convert LF to CRLF"
    96		default y
    97		---help---
    98			Prepend a carriage return before every linefeed that goes into the
    99			syslog.
   100

   102		int "Maximum SYSLOG channels"
   103		default 1
   104		---help---
   105			Maximum number of supported SYSLOG channels.

   107	config SYSLOG_DROPMASK
   108		bool "Channels can drop messages by priority"
   109		default n
   110		---help---
   111			Each SYSLOG channel gets a mask of the message priorities that it
   112			does not write (sc_dropmask).  For example, the console can write
   113			only the urgent messages while the RAMLOG keeps all of them.  See
   114			SYSLOG_CONSOLE_LEVEL.
   115	
   116	config RAMLOG
   117		bool "RAM log device support"
   118		default n
   119		---help---
   120			This is a driver that was intended to support debugging output,
   121			aka syslogging, when the normal serial output is not available.
   122			For example, if you are using a telnet or USB serial console,
   123			the debug output will get lost.  However, the RAMLOG device should
   124			be usable even if system logging is disabled.
   125	
   126			This driver is similar to a pipe in that it saves the debugging
   127			output in a FIFO in RAM.  It differs from a pipe in numerous
   128			details as needed to support logging.
   129	
   130	if RAMLOG
   131	config RAMLOG_NONBLOCKING
   132		bool "RAMLOG non-block reads"
   133		default y
   134		---help---
   135			Reading from the RAMLOG will never block if the RAMLOG is empty.  If the RAMLOG
   136			is empty, then zero is returned (usually interpreted as end-of-file).
   137	
   138	config RAMLOG_POLLTHRESHOLD
   139		int "The threshold value of circular buffer to notify poll waiters"
   140		default 1
   141		---help---
   142			When the length of circular buffer exceeds the threshold value, the poll() will
   143			return POLLIN to all poll waiters.
   144	endif

   146	config SYSLOG_BUFFER
   147		bool "Use buffered output"
   148		default n
   149		---help---
   150			Enables an buffering logic that will be used to serialize debug
   151			output from concurrent tasks. This enables allocation of one buffer
   152			per thread, each of size CONFIG_IOB_BUFSIZE.
   153	
   154			The use of SYSLOG buffering is optional.  If not enabled, however,
   155			then the output from multiple tasks that attempt to generate SYSLOG
   156			output may be interleaved and difficult to read.

   407		default ARCH_LOWPUTC && !SYSLOG_CHAR && !RAMLOG_SYSLOG && !SYSLOG_RPMSG && !SYSLOG_RTT && !SYSLOG_CDCACM && !SYSLOG_CONSOLE
   408		---help---
   409			syslog() interfaces will be present, but all output will go to the
   410			up_putc(ARCH_LOWPUTC == y) or bit-bucket(ARCH_LOWPUTC == n).
   411	
   412	config SYSLOG_CONSOLE_LEVEL
   413		int "Lowest message priority on the console"
   414		default 7
   415		range 0 7
   416		depends on SYSLOG_DROPMASK
   417		depends on SYSLOG_DEFAULT || SYSLOG_CONSOLE
   418		---help---
   419			The console channels (SYSLOG_DEFAULT and SYSLOG_CONSOLE) write
   420			only the messages of this priority and of the more urgent
   421			priorities: 0 is LOG_EMERG, 3 is LOG_ERR, 7 is LOG_DEBUG.  The
   422			other channels (for example the RAMLOG) get all messages.  With
   423			7 (the default), the console gets all messages.
   424	
   425			A message that comes without a priority (for example a write to
   426			/dev/log or output that the interrupt buffer saved) goes to all
   427			channels.
   428	
   429	endif
   430

   432

   443			Size of the console RAM log.  Default: 1024

   489	
   490	menuconfig SYSLOG_FILE
   491		bool "Syslog file output"
   492		default n
   493		select SYSLOG_REGISTER
   494		---help---
   495			Build in support to use a file to collect SYSLOG output.  File SYSLOG
   496			channels differ from other SYSLOG channels in that they cannot be
   497			established until after fully booting and mounting the target file
   498			system.  The function syslog_file_channel() would need to be called
   499			from board-specific bring-up logic AFTER mounting the file system
   500			containing 'devpath'.
   501	
   502			NOTE interrupt level SYSLOG output will be lost in this case unless
   503			the interrupt buffer is used.
   504	
   505	if SYSLOG_FILE
   506	
   507	config SYSLOG_FILE_SEPARATE
   508		bool "Log file separation"
   509		default n
   510		---help---
   511			If enabled, every time the file logger is re-attached, a separator
   512			will be printed in the file.
   513	
   514			This can be useful to easily distinguish between log entries that
   515			belong to different log sessions (e.g. system reboot), and to
   516			indicate that between the separated lines there may be more logs
   517			that were lost.
   518	
   519	config SYSLOG_FILE_ROTATIONS
   520		int "Log file rotations"
   521		default 0
   522		---help---
   523			If enabled (set to a non-zero number), the log file size will be
   524			checked before opening. If it is larger than the specified limit
   525			it will be "rotated", i.e. the old file will be kept as a backup,
   526			and a new empty	file will be created.
   527	
   528			The number of rotations specifies the number of old log files to
   529			keep.
   530	
   531			This option is useful to ensure that log files do not get
   532			huge after prolonged periods of system operation.
   533	
   534	config SYSLOG_FILE_SIZE_LIMIT
   535		int "Log file size limit"
   536		default 524288
   537		depends on SYSLOG_FILE_ROTATIONS > 0
   538		---help---
   539			File size limit when the log is rotated automatically.
   540			If a log file is found larger than this limit, it will
   541			be rotated.
   542	
   543	endif # SYSLOG_FILE
   544	
   545	config CONSOLE_SYSLOG
   546		bool "Use SYSLOG for /dev/console"
   547		default n
   548		depends on DEV_CONSOLE && !SYSLOG_CONSOLE
   549		---help---
   550			Use the syslog logging device as a system console. If this feature is
   551			enabled (along with DEV_CONSOLE), then all console output will be
   552			re-directed to syslog output (syslog_write). This is useful, for
   553			example, if the only console is a Telnet console.  Then in that case,
   554			console output from non-Telnet threads will go to the syslog output.
   555	
   556	config SYSLOG_IOCTL
   557		bool "SYSLOG IOCTL support"
   558		default n
   559		---help---
   560			Enables support for the SYSLOG IOCTL command.  This command is used
   561			to control the behavior of the SYSLOG device. Currently, the supported
   562			commands are SYSLOGIOC_SETFILTER/SYSLOGIOC_GETCHANNELS, which can be
   563			used to set the enable status of different channels
   564	
   565	config SYSLOG_REGISTER
   566		bool "Register syslog channel support"
   567		---help---
   568			This option will support register the syslog channel dynamically.
   569	
   570	endif # SYSLOG
   571	endmenu # System logging

     8	 * this work for additional information regarding copyright ownership.  The
     9	 * ASF licenses this file to you under the Apache License, Version 2.0 (the
    10	 * "License"); you may not use this file except in compliance with the
    11	 * License.  You may obtain a copy of the License at
    12	 *
    13	 *   http://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0
    14	 *
    15	 * Unless required by applicable law or agreed to in writing, software
    16	 * distributed under the License is distributed on an "AS IS" BASIS, WITHOUT
    17	 * WARRANTIES OR CONDITIONS OF ANY KIND, either express or implied.  See the
    18	 * License for the specific language governing permissions and limitations
    19	 * under the License.
    20	 *
    21	 ****************************************************************************/
    22	
    23	/****************************************************************************
    24	 * Included Files
    25	 ****************************************************************************/
    26	
    27	#include <nuttx/config.h>
    28	
    29	#include <stdio.h>
    30	
    31	#include <nuttx/syslog/ramlog.h>
    32	#include <nuttx/syslog/syslog.h>
    33	#include <nuttx/syslog/syslog_rpmsg.h>
    34	
    35	#include "syslog.h"
    36	
    37	#ifndef CONFIG_ARCH_SYSLOG
    38	
    39	/****************************************************************************
    40	 * Public Functions
    41	 ****************************************************************************/
    42	
    43	/****************************************************************************
    44	 * Name: syslog_initialize
    45	 *
    46	 * Description:
    47	 *   One power up, the SYSLOG facility is non-existent or limited to very
    48	 *   low-level output.  This function is called later in the initialization
    49	 *   sequence after full driver support has been initialized.  It installs
    50	 *   the configured SYSLOG drivers and enables full SYSLOGing capability.
    51	 *
    52	 *   This function performs these basic operations:
    53	 *
    54	 *   - Initialize the SYSLOG device
    55	 *   - Call syslog_channel_register() to begin using that device.
    56	 *
    57	 *   If CONFIG_ARCH_SYSLOG is selected, then the architecture-specifica
    58	 *   logic will provide its own SYSLOG device initialize which must include
    59	 *   as a minimum a call to syslog_channel_register() to use the device.
    60	 *
    61	 * Input Parameters:
    62	 *  None
    63	 *
    64	 * Returned Value:
    65	 *   Zero (OK) is returned on success; a negated errno value is returned on
    66	 *   any failure.
    67	 *
    68	 ****************************************************************************/
    69	
    70	int syslog_initialize(void)
    71	{
    72	  int ret = OK;
    73	
    74	#if defined(CONFIG_SYSLOG_CHAR)
    75	  /* Enable use of a character device as the SYSLOG device */
    76	
    77	  syslog_dev_channel();
    78	#elif defined(CONFIG_SYSLOG_CONSOLE)
    79	  /* Use the console device as the SYSLOG device */
    80	
    81	  syslog_console_channel();
    82	#endif
    83	
    84	#ifdef CONFIG_RAMLOG_SYSLOG
    85	  ramlog_syslog_register();
    86	#endif
    87	
    88	#ifdef CONFIG_SYSLOG_CHARDEV
    89	  syslog_register();
    90	#endif
    91	
    92	#ifdef CONFIG_SYSLOG_RPMSG
    93	  syslog_rpmsg_init();
    94	#endif
    95	
    96	#ifdef CONFIG_SYSLOG_RPMSG_SERVER
    97	  syslog_rpmsg_server_init();
    98	#endif
    99	
   100	  return ret;
   101	}
   102	
   103	#endif /* CONFIG_ARCH_SYSLOG */

--- FILE: drivers/syslog/syslog_channel.c ---
     1	/****************************************************************************
     2	 * drivers/syslog/syslog_channel.c
     3	 *

   253	/****************************************************************************
   254	 * Private Functions
   255	 ****************************************************************************/
   256	
   257	#if defined(CONFIG_SYSLOG_DEFAULT) && defined(CONFIG_ARCH_LOWPUTC)
   258	
   259	/****************************************************************************
   260	 * Name: csection_available
   261	 *
   262	 * Description:
   263	 *   Return true if the critical section is available.
   264	 *
   265	 ****************************************************************************/
   266	
   267	static bool csection_available(void)
   268	{
   269	  /* Degrade the critical section in a few cases:
   270	   *
   271	   * a) early in the boot, where tasks are not available
   272	   *
   273	   * b) after a panic, where taking a lock can make the situation worse
   274	   */
   275	
   276	  return OSINIT_TASK_READY() && g_nx_initstate != OSINIT_PANIC;
   277	}
   278	
   279	#endif /* defined(CONFIG_SYSLOG_DEFAULT) && defined(CONFIG_ARCH_LOWPUTC) */
   280	
   281	/****************************************************************************
   282	 * Name: syslog_default_putc
   283	 *
   284	 * Description:
   285	 *   If the arch supports a low-level putc function, output will be
   286	 *   redirected there. Else acts as a dummy, no-nothing channel.
   287	 *
   288	 ****************************************************************************/
   289	
   290	#ifdef CONFIG_SYSLOG_DEFAULT
   291	static int syslog_default_putc(FAR syslog_channel_t *channel, int ch)
   292	{
   293	#  ifdef CONFIG_ARCH_LOWPUTC
   294	  if (csection_available())
   295	    {
   296	      /* See https://github.com/apache/nuttx/issues/14662
   297	       * about what this critical section is for.
   298	       */
   299	
   300	      irqstate_t flags = enter_critical_section();
   301	
   302	      up_putc(ch);
   303	      leave_critical_section(flags);
   304	    }
   305	  else
   306	    {
   307	      up_putc(ch);
   308	    }
   309	#  endif
   310	
   311	  UNUSED(channel);
   312	  return ch;
   313	}
   314	
   315	static ssize_t syslog_default_write(FAR syslog_channel_t *channel,
   316	                                    FAR const char *buffer, size_t buflen)
   317	{
   318	#  ifdef CONFIG_ARCH_LOWPUTC
   319	  if (csection_available())
   320	    {
   321	      /* See https://github.com/apache/nuttx/issues/14662
   322	       * about what this critical section is for.
   323	       */
   324	
   325	      irqstate_t flags = enter_critical_section();
   326	
   327	      up_nputs(buffer, buflen);
   328	      leave_critical_section(flags);
   329	    }
   330	  else
   331	    {
   332	      up_nputs(buffer, buflen);
   333	    }
   334	#  endif
   335	
   336	  UNUSED(channel);
   337	  return buflen;
   338	}
   339	#endif
   340	
   341	#ifdef CONFIG_SYSLOG_CDCACM
   342	static int syslog_cdcacm_putc(FAR syslog_channel_t *channel, int ch)
   343	{
   344	  char tmp;
   345	
   346	  tmp = ch;
   347	  cdcacm_write(&tmp, 1);
   348	
   349	  UNUSED(channel);
   350	  return ch;
   351	}
   352	
   353	static ssize_t syslog_cdcacm_write(FAR syslog_channel_t *channel,
   354	                                   FAR const char *buffer, size_t buflen)
   355	{
   356	  UNUSED(channel);
   357	
   358	  return cdcacm_write(buffer, buflen);
   359	}
   360	#endif
   361	
   362	/****************************************************************************
   363	 * Public Functions
   364	 ****************************************************************************/
   365	
   366	/****************************************************************************
   367	 * Name: syslog_channel
   368	 *
   369	 * Description:
   370	 *   Configure the SYSLOGging function to use the provided channel to
   371	 *   generate SYSLOG output.
   372	 *
   373	 * Input Parameters:
   374	 *   channel - Provides the interface to the channel to be used.
   375	 *
   376	 * Returned Value:
   377	 *   Zero (OK) is returned on success.  A negated errno value is returned
   378	 *   on any failure.
   379	 *
   380	 ****************************************************************************/
   381	
   382	#ifdef CONFIG_SYSLOG_REGISTER
   383	int syslog_channel_register(FAR syslog_channel_t *channel)
   384	{
   385	  DEBUGASSERT(channel != NULL);
   386	
   387	  if (channel != NULL)
   388	    {
   389	#if CONFIG_SYSLOG_MAX_CHANNELS == 1
   390	      g_syslog_channel[0] = channel;
   391	      return OK;
   392	#else
   393	      int i;
   394	
   395	      for (i = 0; i < CONFIG_SYSLOG_MAX_CHANNELS; i++)
   396	        {
   397	          if (g_syslog_channel[i] == NULL)
   398	            {
   399	#  ifdef CONFIG_SYSLOG_IOCTL
   400	              if (channel->sc_name[0] == '\0')
   401	                {
   402	                  snprintf(channel->sc_name, sizeof(channel->sc_name),
   403	                           "channel-%p", channel->sc_ops);
   404	                }
   405	#  endif
   406	
   407	              g_syslog_channel[i] = channel;
   408	              return OK;
   409	            }
   410	          else if (g_syslog_channel[i] == channel)
   411	            {
   412	              return OK;
   413	            }
   414	        }
   415	#endif
   416	    }
   417	
   418	  return -EINVAL;
   419	}
   420	
   421	/****************************************************************************
   422	 * Name: syslog_channel_unregister
   423	 *
   424	 * Description:
   425	 *   Removes an already configured SYSLOG channel from the list of used
   426	 *   channels.
   427	 *
   428	 * Input Parameters:
   429	 *   channel - Provides the interface to the channel to be removed.
   430	 *
   431	 * Returned Value:
   432	 *   Zero (OK) is returned on success.  A negated errno value is returned
   433	 *   on any failure.
   434	 *
   435	 ****************************************************************************/
   436	
   437	int syslog_channel_unregister(FAR syslog_channel_t *channel)
   438	{
   439	  int i;
   440	
   441	  DEBUGASSERT(channel != NULL);
   442	
   443	  if (channel != NULL)
   444	    {
   445	      for (i = 0; i < CONFIG_SYSLOG_MAX_CHANNELS; i++)
   446	        {
   447	          if (g_syslog_channel[i] == channel)
   448	            {
   449	              /* Get the rest of the channels one position back
   450	               * to ensure that there are no holes in the list.
   451	               */
   452	
   453	              while (i < (CONFIG_SYSLOG_MAX_CHANNELS - 1) &&
   454	                     g_syslog_channel[i + 1] != NULL)
   455	                {
   456	                  g_syslog_channel[i] = g_syslog_channel[i + 1];
   457	                  i++;
   458	                }
   459	
   460	              g_syslog_channel[i] = NULL;
   461	
   462	              /* The channel is now removed from the list and its driver
   463	               * can be safely uninitialized.
   464	               */
   465	
   466	              if (channel->sc_ops->sc_close)
   467	                {
   468	                  channel->sc_ops->sc_close(channel);
   469	                }
   470	
   471	              return OK;
   472	            }
   473	        }
   474	    }
   475	
   476	  return -EINVAL;
   477	}
   478	#endif

    57	#ifdef CONFIG_RAMLOG
    58	
    59	/****************************************************************************
    60	 * Pre-processor Definitions
    61	 ****************************************************************************/
    62	
    63	#define RAMLOG_MAGIC_INIT  0x12340000
    64	#define RAMLOG_MAGIC_READY (0x56780000 | sizeof(struct ramlog_header_s))
    65	
    66	/****************************************************************************
    67	 * Private Types
    68	 ****************************************************************************/
    69	
    70	struct ramlog_ratelimit_s
    71	{
    72	  unsigned int  interval; /* The interval in seconds */
    73	  unsigned int  burst;    /* The max allowed note number during interval */
    74	  unsigned int  printed;  /* The number of printed note during interval */
    75	  unsigned long begin;    /* The timestamp in seconds */
    76	};
    77	
    78	struct ramlog_header_s
    79	{
    80	  uint32_t          rl_magic;    /* The rl_magic number for ramlog buffer init */
    81	  volatile uint32_t rl_head;     /* The head index (where data is added,natural growth) */
    82	  char              rl_buffer[]; /* Circular RAM buffer */
    83	};
    84	
    85	struct ramlog_user_s
    86	{
    87	  struct list_node  rl_node;       /* The list_node of reader */
    88	  volatile uint32_t rl_tail;       /* The tail index (where data is removed) */
    89	  uint32_t          rl_threashold; /* The threshold of the reader to read log */
    90	#ifndef CONFIG_RAMLOG_NONBLOCKING
    91	  sem_t             rl_waitsem;    /* Used to wait for data */
    92	#endif
    93	
    94	  /* The following the poll structures of threads waiting for driver events.
    95	   * The 'struct pollfd_s' reference for each open is also  retained in the
    96	   * f_priv field of the 'struct file'.
    97	   */
    98	
    99	  FAR struct pollfd_s *rl_fds;
   100	};
   101	
   102	struct ramlog_dev_s
   103	{
   104	  /* The following is the header of the RAM buffer,
   105	   * Store the RAM BUFFER init rl_magic number,
   106	   * and read/write pointers
   107	   */
   108	
   109	  FAR struct ramlog_header_s *rl_header;
   110	
   111	  uint32_t                   rl_bufsize;   /* Size of the circular buffer */
   112	  struct list_node           rl_list;      /* The list of ramlog_user_s */
   113	  struct ramlog_ratelimit_s  rl_ratelimit; /* The ratelimit for ramlog */
   114	};
   115	
   116	/****************************************************************************
   117	 * Private Function Prototypes
   118	 ****************************************************************************/
   119	
   120	/* Helper functions */
   121	
   122	#ifndef CONFIG_RAMLOG_NONBLOCKING
   123	static void    ramlog_readnotify(FAR struct ramlog_dev_s *priv);
   124	#endif
   125	static void    ramlog_pollnotify(FAR struct ramlog_dev_s *priv);
   126	
   127	/* Character driver methods */
   128	
   129	static int     ramlog_file_open(FAR struct file *filep);
   130	static int     ramlog_file_close(FAR struct file *filep);
   131	static ssize_t ramlog_file_read(FAR struct file *filep, FAR char *buffer,
   132	                                size_t buflen);
   133	static ssize_t ramlog_file_write(FAR struct file *filep,
   134	                                 FAR const char *buffer, size_t buflen);
   135	static int     ramlog_file_ioctl(FAR struct file *filep, int cmd,
   136	                                 unsigned long arg);
   137	static int     ramlog_file_poll(FAR struct file *filep,
   138	                                FAR struct pollfd_s *fds, bool setup);
   139	
   140	/****************************************************************************
   141	 * Private Data
   142	 ****************************************************************************/
   143	
   144	static const struct file_operations g_ramlogfops =
   145	{
   146	  ramlog_file_open,  /* open */
   147	  ramlog_file_close, /* close */
   148	  ramlog_file_read,  /* read */
   149	  ramlog_file_write, /* write */
   150	  NULL,              /* seek */
   151	  ramlog_file_ioctl, /* ioctl */
   152	  NULL,              /* mmap */
   153	  NULL,              /* truncate */
   154	  ramlog_file_poll   /* poll */
   155	};
   156	
   157	/* This is the pre-allocated buffer used for the console RAM log and/or
   158	 * for the syslogging function.
   159	 */
   160	
   161	#ifdef CONFIG_RAMLOG_SYSLOG
   162	#  ifdef RAMLOG_BUFFER_SECTION
   163	static uint32_t g_sysbuffer[CONFIG_RAMLOG_BUFSIZE / 4]
   164	                       locate_data(RAMLOG_BUFFER_SECTION);
   165	#  else
   166	static uint32_t g_sysbuffer[CONFIG_RAMLOG_BUFSIZE / 4];
   167	#  endif
   168	
   169	/* This is the device structure for the console or syslogging function.  It
   170	 * must be statically initialized because the RAMLOG ramlog_putc function
   171	 * could be called before the driver initialization logic executes.
   172	 */
   173	
   174	static struct ramlog_dev_s g_sysdev =
   175	{
   176	  (FAR struct ramlog_header_s *)g_sysbuffer,            /* rl_buffer */
   177	  sizeof(g_sysbuffer) - sizeof(struct ramlog_header_s), /* rl_bufsize */
   178	  LIST_INITIAL_VALUE(g_sysdev.rl_list)                  /* rl_list */
   179	};
   180	
   181	#endif
   182	
   183	/****************************************************************************
   184	 * Private Functions
   185	 ****************************************************************************/
   186	
   187	/****************************************************************************
   188	 * Name: ramlog_ratelimit
   189	 *
   190	 * Description:
   191	 *   Check whether the log is limited.
   192	 *
   193	 * Input Parameters:
   194	 *   dev - The pointer of ramlog device.
   195	 *
   196	 * Returned Value:
   197	 *   True is returned if the log is limited.
   198	 *
   199	 ****************************************************************************/
   200	
   201	static bool ramlog_ratelimit(FAR struct ramlog_dev_s *dev)
   202	{
   203	  bool ret;
   204	  clock_t ticks;
   205	  uint32_t seconds;
   206	  FAR struct ramlog_ratelimit_s *limit;
   207	
   208	  limit = &dev->rl_ratelimit;
   209	
   210	  if (limit->interval == 0)
   211	    {
   212	      return false;
   213	    }
   214	
   215	  ticks = clock_systime_ticks();
   216	  seconds = ticks * CONFIG_USEC_PER_TICK / 1000000;
   217	
   218	  if (limit->begin == 0)
   219	    {
   220	      limit->begin = seconds;
   221	    }
   222	
   223	  /* Reset statistical information */
   224	
   225	  if ((seconds - limit->begin) >= limit->interval)
   226	    {
   227	      limit->begin = seconds;
   228	      limit->printed = 0;
   229	    }
   230	
   231	  /* Check if the note is limited */
   232	
   233	  if (limit->burst && limit->burst > limit->printed)
   234	    {
   235	      limit->printed++;
   236	      ret = false;
   237	    }
   238	  else
   239	    {
   240	      ret = true;
   241	    }
   242	
   243	  return ret;
   244	}
   245	
   246	/****************************************************************************
   247	 * Name: ramlog_bufferused
   248	 ****************************************************************************/
   249	
   250	static uint32_t ramlog_bufferused(FAR struct ramlog_dev_s *priv,
   251	                                  FAR struct ramlog_user_s *upriv)
   252	{
   253	  uint32_t used = priv->rl_header->rl_head - upriv->rl_tail;
   254	
   255	  return used > priv->rl_bufsize ? priv->rl_bufsize : used;
   256	}
   257	
   258	/****************************************************************************
   259	 * Name: ramlog_readnotify
   260	 ****************************************************************************/
   261	
   262	#ifndef CONFIG_RAMLOG_NONBLOCKING
   263	static void ramlog_readnotify(FAR struct ramlog_dev_s *priv)
   264	{
   265	  FAR struct ramlog_user_s *upriv;
   266	
   267	  /* Notify all waiting readers that they can read from the FIFO */
   268	
   269	  list_for_every_entry(&priv->rl_list, upriv, struct ramlog_user_s, rl_node)
   270	    {
   271	      for (; ; )
   272	        {
   273	          int semcount = 0;
   274	
   275	          nxsem_get_value(&upriv->rl_waitsem, &semcount);
   276	          if (semcount >= 0)
   277	            {
   278	              break;
   279	            }
   280	
   281	          nxsem_post(&upriv->rl_waitsem);
   282	        }
   283	    }
   284	}
   285	#endif
   286	
   287	/****************************************************************************
   288	 * Name: ramlog_pollnotify
   289	 ****************************************************************************/
   290	
   291	static void ramlog_pollnotify(FAR struct ramlog_dev_s *priv)
   292	{
   293	  FAR struct ramlog_user_s *upriv;
   294	
   295	  /* This function may be called from an interrupt handler */
   296	
   297	  list_for_every_entry(&priv->rl_list, upriv, struct ramlog_user_s, rl_node)
   298	    {
   299	      if (ramlog_bufferused(priv, upriv) >= upriv->rl_threashold)
   300	        {
   301	          /* Notify all poll/select waiters that they can read from
   302	           * the FIFO
   303	           */
   304	
   305	          poll_notify(&upriv->rl_fds, 1, POLLIN);
   306	        }
   307	    }
   308	}
   309	
   310	/****************************************************************************
   311	 * Name: ramlog_flush
   312	 ****************************************************************************/
   313	
   314	static void ramlog_bufferflush(FAR struct ramlog_dev_s *priv)
   315	{
   316	  FAR struct ramlog_user_s *upriv;
   317	
   318	  priv->rl_header->rl_head = 0;
   319	  list_for_every_entry(&priv->rl_list, upriv, struct ramlog_user_s, rl_node)
   320	    {
   321	      upriv->rl_tail = 0;
   322	    }
   323	}
   324	
   325	/****************************************************************************
   326	 * Name: ramlog_copybuf
   327	 ****************************************************************************/
   328	
   329	static void ramlog_copybuf(FAR struct ramlog_dev_s *priv,
   330	                          FAR const char *buffer, size_t len)
   331	{
   332	  FAR struct ramlog_header_s *header = priv->rl_header;
   333	  FAR char *buf = header->rl_buffer;
   334	  uint32_t offset;
   335	  uint32_t tail;
   336	
   337	  if (len <= 0)
   338	    {
   339	      return;
   340	    }
   341	
   342	  offset = header->rl_head % priv->rl_bufsize;
   343	  tail = priv->rl_bufsize - offset;
   344	
   345	  if (len > tail)
   346	    {
   347	      memcpy(&buf[offset], buffer, tail);
   348	      memcpy(buf, buffer + tail, len - tail);
   349	    }
   350	  else
   351	    {
   352	      memcpy(&buf[offset], buffer, len);
   353	    }
   354	
   355	  header->rl_head += len;
   356	}
   357	
   358	/****************************************************************************
   359	 * Name: ramlog_addbuf
   360	 ****************************************************************************/
   361	
   362	static ssize_t ramlog_addbuf(FAR struct ramlog_dev_s *priv,
   363	                             FAR const char *buffer, size_t len)
   364	{
   365	#ifdef CONFIG_RAMLOG_SYSLOG
   366	  FAR struct ramlog_header_s *header = priv->rl_header;
   367	#endif
   368	  size_t buflen = len;
   369	  irqstate_t flags;
   370	
   371	  /* Disable interrupts (in case we are NOT called from interrupt handler).
   372	   *
   373	   * Not enter_critical_section(): it consults the current task, and on
   374	   * some ports this channel takes syslog output before the task lists
   375	   * exist.  Masking interrupts protects the buffer just as well while
   376	   * there is only one thread of control.
   377	   */
   378	
   379	  if (OSINIT_TASK_READY())
   380	    {
   381	      flags = enter_critical_section();
   382	    }
   383	  else
   384	    {
   385	      flags = irq_save();
   386	    }
   387	
   388	  if (ramlog_ratelimit(priv))
   389	    {
   390	      if (OSINIT_TASK_READY())
   391	        {
   392	          leave_critical_section(flags);
   393	        }
   394	      else
   395	        {
   396	          irq_restore(flags);
   397	        }
   398	
   399	      return len;
   400	    }
   401	
   402	#ifdef CONFIG_RAMLOG_SYSLOG
   403	  if (header->rl_magic != RAMLOG_MAGIC_READY && priv == &g_sysdev)
   404	    {
   405	      memset(header, 0, sizeof(g_sysbuffer));
   406	      header->rl_magic = RAMLOG_MAGIC_READY;
   407	    }
   408	#endif
   409	
   410	  if (buflen > priv->rl_bufsize)
   411	    {
   412	      buffer += buflen - priv->rl_bufsize;
   413	      buflen = priv->rl_bufsize;
   414	    }
   415	
   416	  ramlog_copybuf(priv, buffer, buflen);
   417	
   418	  /* Was anything written?  Notify only once the scheduler exists; early
   419	   * boot output reaches here long before it does, and locking a scheduler
   420	   * that is not there yet faults.  The bytes land in the buffer either
   421	   * way.
   422	   */
   423	
   424	  if (len > 0 && OSINIT_OS_READY())
   425	    {
   426	      /* Lock the scheduler do NOT switch out */
   427	
   428	      if (!up_interrupt_context())
   429	        {
   430	          sched_lock();
   431	        }
   432	
   433	#ifndef CONFIG_RAMLOG_NONBLOCKING
   434	      /* Are there threads waiting for read data? */
   435	
   436	      ramlog_readnotify(priv);
   437	#endif
   438	      /* Notify all poll/select waiters that they can read from the FIFO */
   439	
   440	      ramlog_pollnotify(priv);
   441	
   442	      /* Unlock the scheduler */
   443	
   444	      if (!up_interrupt_context())
   445	        {
   446	          sched_unlock();
   447	        }
   448	    }
   449	
   450	  /* We always have to return the number of bytes requested and NOT the
   451	   * number of bytes that were actually written.  Otherwise, callers
   452	   * probably retry, causing same error condition again.
   453	   */
   454	
   455	  if (OSINIT_TASK_READY())
   456	    {
   457	      leave_critical_section(flags);
   458	    }
   459	  else
   460	    {
   461	      irq_restore(flags);
   462	    }
   463	
   464	  return len;
   465	}
   466	
   467	/****************************************************************************
   468	 * Name: ramlog_read
   469	 ****************************************************************************/
   470	
   471	static ssize_t ramlog_file_read(FAR struct file *filep, FAR char *buffer,
   472	                                size_t len)
   473	{
   474	  FAR struct inode *inode = filep->f_inode;
   475	  FAR struct ramlog_dev_s *priv = inode->i_private;
   476	  FAR struct ramlog_header_s *header = priv->rl_header;
   477	  FAR struct ramlog_user_s *upriv = filep->f_priv;
   478	  irqstate_t flags;
   479	  uint32_t ncopy;
   480	  ssize_t nread;
   481	  uint32_t tail;
   482	  uint32_t pos;
   483	
   484	  /* Get exclusive access to the rl_tail index */
   485	
   486	  flags = enter_critical_section();
   487	
   488	  /* Loop until something is read */
   489	
   490	  for (nread = 0; (size_t)nread < len; )
   491	    {
   492	      /* Get the next byte from the buffer */
   493	
   494	      if (header->rl_head == upriv->rl_tail)
   495	        {
   496	          /* The circular buffer is empty. */
   497	
   498	          /* If the function be called from an interrupt handler,
   499	           * then just break out of the loop.
   500	           */
   501	
   502	          if (up_interrupt_context())
   503	            {
   504	              break;
   505	            }
   506	
   507	#ifdef CONFIG_RAMLOG_NONBLOCKING
   508	          /* Return what we have (with zero mean the end-of-file) */
   509	
   510	          break;
   511	#else
   512	          int ret;
   513	
   514	          /* Did we read anything? */
   515	
   516	          if (nread > 0)
   517	            {
   518	              /* Yes.. break out to return what we have. */
   519	
   520	              break;
   521	            }
   522	
   523	          /* If the driver was opened with O_NONBLOCK option, then don't
   524	           * wait.
   525	           */
   526	
   527	          if (filep->f_oflags & O_NONBLOCK)
   528	            {
   529	              nread = -EAGAIN;
   530	              break;
   531	            }
   532	
   533	          /* We may now be preempted!  But that should be okay because we
   534	           * have already incremented nwaiters.  Pre-emptions is disabled
   535	           * but will be re-enabled while we are waiting.
   536	           */
   537	
   538	          ret = nxsem_wait(&upriv->rl_waitsem);
   539	
   540	          /* Did we successfully get the rl_waitsem? */
   541	
   542	          if (ret < 0)
   543	            {
   544	              /* No.. nxsem_wait's failed. */
   545	
   546	              /* Return the error. We did handle the case where we read
   547	               * anything already before waiting.
   548	               */
   549	
   550	              return ret;
   551	            }
   552	#endif /* CONFIG_RAMLOG_NONBLOCKING */
   553	        }
   554	      else
   555	        {
   556	          /* Determine whether the read pointer is overwritten */
   557	
   558	          if (header->rl_head - upriv->rl_tail > priv->rl_bufsize)
   559	            {
   560	              upriv->rl_tail = header->rl_head - priv->rl_bufsize;
   561	            }
   562	
   563	          /* The circular buffer is not empty, get the next byte from the
   564	           * tail index.
   565	           */
   566	
   567	          pos = upriv->rl_tail % priv->rl_bufsize;
   568	          ncopy = header->rl_head - upriv->rl_tail;
   569	          tail = priv->rl_bufsize - pos;
   570	
   571	          if (ncopy > len - nread)
   572	            {
   573	              ncopy = len - nread;
   574	            }
   575	
   576	          if (ncopy > tail)
   577	            {
   578	              memcpy(&buffer[nread], &header->rl_buffer[pos], tail);
   579	              memcpy(&buffer[nread + tail], header->rl_buffer, ncopy - tail);
   580	            }
   581	          else
   582	            {
   583	              memcpy(&buffer[nread], &header->rl_buffer[pos], ncopy);
   584	            }
   585	
   586	          upriv->rl_tail += ncopy;
   587	          nread += ncopy;
   588	        }
   589	    }
   590	
   591	  leave_critical_section(flags);
   592	
   593	  /* Return the number of characters actually read */
   594	
   595	  return nread;
   596	}
   597	
   598	/****************************************************************************
   599	 * Name: ramlog_file_write
   600	 ****************************************************************************/
   601	
   602	static ssize_t ramlog_file_write(FAR struct file *filep,
   603	                                 FAR const char *buffer, size_t len)
   604	{
   605	  FAR struct inode *inode = filep->f_inode;
   606	  FAR struct ramlog_dev_s *priv = inode->i_private;
   607	
   608	  return ramlog_addbuf(priv, buffer, len);
   609	}
   610	
   611	/****************************************************************************
   612	 * Name: ramlog_file_ioctl
   613	 ****************************************************************************/
   614	
   615	static int ramlog_file_ioctl(FAR struct file *filep, int cmd,
   616	                             unsigned long arg)
   617	{
   618	  FAR struct inode *inode = filep->f_inode;
   619	  FAR struct ramlog_dev_s *priv = inode->i_private;
   620	  FAR struct ramlog_user_s *upriv = filep->f_priv;
   621	  irqstate_t flags;
   622	  int ret = 0;
   623	
   624	  flags = enter_critical_section();
   625	
   626	  switch (cmd)
   627	    {
   628	      case FIONREAD:
   629	        *(FAR int *)((uintptr_t)arg) = ramlog_bufferused(priv, upriv);
   630	        break;
   631	      case PIPEIOC_POLLINTHRD:
   632	        upriv->rl_threashold = (uint32_t)arg;
   633	        break;
   634	      case PIPEIOC_GETSIZE:
   635	        if (arg == 0)
   636	          {
   637	            ret = -EINVAL;
   638	          }
   639	        else
   640	          {
   641	            *(FAR unsigned int *)arg = priv->rl_bufsize;
   642	          }
   643	        break;
   644	      case BIOC_FLUSH:
   645	        ramlog_bufferflush(priv);
   646	        break;
   647	
   648	      case SYSLOGIOC_SETRATELIMIT:
   649	        if (arg == 0)
   650	          {
   651	            ret = -EINVAL;
   652	          }
   653	        else
   654	          {
   655	            FAR struct syslog_ratelimit_s *limit =
   656	              (FAR struct syslog_ratelimit_s *)arg;
   657	
   658	            priv->rl_ratelimit.interval = limit->interval;
   659	            priv->rl_ratelimit.burst = limit->burst;
   660	            ret = 0;
   661	          }
   662	        break;
   663	
   664	      case SYSLOGIOC_GETRATELIMIT:
   665	        if (arg == 0)
   666	          {
   667	            ret = -EINVAL;
   668	          }
   669	        else
   670	          {
   671	            FAR struct syslog_ratelimit_s *limit =
   672	              (FAR struct syslog_ratelimit_s *)arg;
   673	
   674	            limit->interval = priv->rl_ratelimit.interval;
   675	            limit->burst = priv->rl_ratelimit.burst;
   676	            ret = 0;
   677	          }
   678	        break;
   679	
   680	      default:
   681	        ret = -ENOTTY;
   682	        break;
   683	    }
   684	
   685	  leave_critical_section(flags);
   686	  return ret;
   687	}
   688	
   689	/****************************************************************************
   690	 * Name: ramlog_file_poll
   691	 ****************************************************************************/
   692	
   693	static int ramlog_file_poll(FAR struct file *filep, FAR struct pollfd_s *fds,
   694	                            bool setup)
   695	{
   696	  FAR struct inode *inode = filep->f_inode;
   697	  FAR struct ramlog_dev_s *priv = inode->i_private;
   698	  FAR struct ramlog_user_s *upriv = filep->f_priv;
   699	  pollevent_t eventset = POLLOUT;
   700	  irqstate_t flags;
   701	
   702	  /* Get exclusive access to the poll structures */
   703	
   704	  flags = enter_critical_section();
   705	
   706	  /* Are we setting up the poll?  Or tearing it down? */
   707	
   708	  if (setup)
   709	    {
   710	      /* This is a request to set up the poll.  Find an available
   711	       * slot for the poll structure reference.
   712	       */
   713	
   714	      if (!upriv->rl_fds)
   715	        {
   716	          upriv->rl_fds = fds;
   717	          fds->priv     = &upriv->rl_fds;
   718	        }
   719	
   720	      /* Should immediately notify on any of the requested events? */
   721	
   722	      /* Check if the receive buffer is not empty. */
   723	
   724	      if (ramlog_bufferused(priv, upriv) >= upriv->rl_threashold)
   725	        {
   726	          eventset |= POLLIN;
   727	        }
   728	
   729	      poll_notify(&fds, 1, eventset);
   730	    }
   731	  else if (fds->priv)
   732	    {
   733	      /* This is a request to tear down the poll. */
   734	
   735	      FAR struct pollfd_s **slot = (FAR struct pollfd_s **)fds->priv;
   736	
   737	      /* Remove all memory of the poll setup */
   738	
   739	      *slot     = NULL;
   740	      fds->priv = NULL;
   741	    }
   742	
   743	  leave_critical_section(flags);
   744	  return 0;
   745	}
   746	
   747	/****************************************************************************
   748	 * Name: ramlog_file_open
   749	 ****************************************************************************/
   750	
   751	static int ramlog_file_open(FAR struct file *filep)
   752	{
   753	  FAR struct inode *inode = filep->f_inode;
   754	  FAR struct ramlog_dev_s *priv = inode->i_private;
   755	  FAR struct ramlog_header_s *header = priv->rl_header;
   756	  FAR struct ramlog_user_s *upriv;
   757	  irqstate_t flags;
   758	
   759	  /* Get exclusive access to the rl_tail index */
   760	
   761	  upriv = kmm_zalloc(sizeof(FAR struct ramlog_user_s));
   762	  if (upriv == NULL)
   763	    {
   764	      return -ENOMEM;
   765	    }
   766	
   767	  upriv->rl_threashold = CONFIG_RAMLOG_POLLTHRESHOLD;
   768	#ifndef CONFIG_RAMLOG_NONBLOCKING
   769	  nxsem_init(&upriv->rl_waitsem, 0, 0);
   770	#endif
   771	
   772	  flags = enter_critical_section();
   773	  list_add_tail(&priv->rl_list, &upriv->rl_node);
   774	  upriv->rl_tail = header->rl_head > priv->rl_bufsize ?
   775	                   header->rl_head - priv->rl_bufsize : 0;
   776	  leave_critical_section(flags);
   777	
   778	  filep->f_priv = upriv;
   779	  return 0;
   780	}
   781	
   782	/****************************************************************************
   783	 * Name: ramlog_file_close
   784	 ****************************************************************************/
   785	
   786	static int ramlog_file_close(FAR struct file *filep)
   787	{
   788	  FAR struct ramlog_user_s *upriv = filep->f_priv;
   789	  irqstate_t flags;
   790	
   791	  /* Get exclusive access to the rl_tail index */
   792	
   793	  flags = enter_critical_section();
   794	  list_delete(&upriv->rl_node);
   795	  leave_critical_section(flags);
   796	
   797	#ifndef CONFIG_RAMLOG_NONBLOCKING
   798	  nxsem_destroy(&upriv->rl_waitsem);
   799	#endif
   800	  kmm_free(upriv);
   801	  return 0;
   802	}
   803	
   804	/****************************************************************************
   805	 * Public Functions
   806	 ****************************************************************************/
   807	
   808	/****************************************************************************
   809	 * Name: ramlog_register
   810	 *
   811	 * Description:
   812	 *   Create the RAM logging device and register it at the specified path.
   813	 *
   814	 ****************************************************************************/
   815	
   816	int ramlog_register(FAR const char *devpath, FAR char *buffer, size_t buflen)
   817	{
   818	  FAR struct ramlog_dev_s *priv;
   819	  int ret = -ENOMEM;
   820	
   821	  /* Sanity checking */
   822	
   823	  DEBUGASSERT(devpath && buffer && buflen > sizeof(struct ramlog_header_s));
   824	
   825	  /* Allocate a RAM logging device structure */
   826	
   827	  priv = kmm_zalloc(sizeof(struct ramlog_dev_s));
   828	  if (priv != NULL)
   829	    {
   830	      /* Initialize the non-zero values in the RAM logging device structure */
   831	
   832	      list_initialize(&priv->rl_list);
   833	      priv->rl_bufsize = buflen - sizeof(struct ramlog_header_s);
   834	      priv->rl_header = (FAR struct ramlog_header_s *)buffer;
   835	
   836	      /* Register the character driver */
   837	
   838	      ret = register_driver(devpath, &g_ramlogfops, 0600, priv);
   839	      if (ret < 0)
   840	        {
   841	          kmm_free(priv);
   842	        }
   843	    }
   844	
   845	  return ret;
   846	}
   847	
   848	/****************************************************************************
   849	 * Name: ramlog_syslog_register
   850	 *
   851	 * Description:
   852	 *   Use a pre-allocated RAM logging device and register it at the path
   853	 *   specified by CONFIG_RAMLOG_SYSLOG
   854	 *
   855	 ****************************************************************************/
   856	
   857	#ifdef CONFIG_RAMLOG_SYSLOG
   858	void ramlog_syslog_register(void)
   859	{
   860	  /* Register the syslog character driver */
   861	
   862	  register_driver(CONFIG_SYSLOG_DEVPATH, &g_ramlogfops, 0600, &g_sysdev);
   863	}
   864	#endif
   865	
   866	/****************************************************************************
   867	 * Name: ramlog_putc
   868	 *
   869	 * Description:
   870	 *   This is the low-level system logging interface.
   871	 *
   872	 ****************************************************************************/
   873	
   874	#ifdef CONFIG_RAMLOG_SYSLOG
   875	int ramlog_putc(FAR syslog_channel_t *channel, int ch)
   876	{
   877	  char cch = ch;
   878	
   879	  UNUSED(channel);
   880	
   881	  /* Add the character to the RAMLOG */
   882	
   883	  ramlog_addbuf(&g_sysdev, &cch, 1);
   884	
   885	  /* Return the character added on success */
   886	
   887	  return ch;
   888	}
   889	
   890	ssize_t ramlog_write(FAR syslog_channel_t *channel,
   891	                     FAR const char *buffer, size_t buflen)
   892	{
   893	  return ramlog_addbuf(&g_sysdev, buffer, buflen);
   894	}
   895	#endif
   896	
   897	#endif /* CONFIG_RAMLOG */
